# chatgpt response

Your `math domain error` is coming from this pattern:

```py
u = (random.random() * 65536.0 + 0.5) / 65536.0
theta = acos(1 - 2u)
```

Because `random.random()` can be extremely close to `1.0`, the expression can push `u` slightly **above 1**, making `1 - 2u < -1`, and `acos()` throws.

Fix: sample `u` in `(0,1)` safely **or clamp the acos argument into [-1, 1]** (do both and you’ll never see it again).

Below is **full, notebook-safe code** (no argparse) implementing your **shared RW null pool** run, plus top-K PDB export, KS sanity checks, and stable empirical p-values.

---

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
SHA-256 TOPOLOGICAL MAPPER — Batch Experiment (Shared Null Pool)

What it does
------------
1) For each message in a batch:
   - Extract SHA-256 single-block T1 trace (64 rounds)
   - Map T1 -> 3D isotropic walk (N=64 coords)
   - Compute metrics: Rg, Ree, r_rw, r_ee, contact_density
   - Compare to a SHARED isotropic random-walk null pool (RW_POOL_SIZE samples)
   - Report empirical p-values: p(Ree small) and p(r_ee large)

2) Summarize:
   - fraction with p(Ree) <= 0.01 and <= 0.001
   - p-value quantiles
   - KS sanity checks vs shared null
   - Top-K most-closed messages
   - Optional PDB export for top-K

No external deps.
"""

import math
import struct
import random
import os
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right
from typing import List, Tuple, Dict

# =========================
# CONFIG (EDIT THESE)
# =========================

BOND_LENGTH = 3.8
CONTACT_CUTOFF = 8.0
MIN_SEQ_SEP = 4

BATCH_MESSAGES = 200
MODE = "random_bytes"      # "random_bytes" or "words"
RANDOM_MSG_LEN = 8

# Shared null pool size (bigger = finer p-value resolution)
RW_POOL_SIZE = 100_000

# Seeds
SEED_MESSAGES = 123
SEED_NULL_POOL = 42

# Export PDBs for top-K most closed
EXPORT_TOP_K_PDB = 10
EXPORT_DIR = "pdb_exports"

# Thresholds
P_CLOSURE_THRESH = 0.01
P_TIGHT_THRESH = 0.001

# =========================
# END CONFIG
# =========================

MASK32 = 0xFFFFFFFF

K = [
    0x428a2f98, 0x71374491, 0xb5c0fbcf, 0xe9b5dba5, 0x3956c25b, 0x59f111f1, 0x923f82a4, 0xab1c5ed5,
    0xd807aa98, 0x12835b01, 0x243185be, 0x550c7dc3, 0x72be5d74, 0x80deb1fe, 0x9bdc06a7, 0xc19bf174,
    0xe49b69c1, 0xefbe4786, 0x0fc19dc6, 0x240ca1cc, 0x2de92c6f, 0x4a7484aa, 0x5cb0a9dc, 0x76f988da,
    0x983e5152, 0xa831c66d, 0xb00327c8, 0xbf597fc7, 0xc6e00bf3, 0xd5a79147, 0x06ca6351, 0x14292967,
    0x27b70a85, 0x2e1b2138, 0x4d2c6dfc, 0x53380d13, 0x650a7354, 0x766a0abb, 0x81c2c92e, 0x92722c85,
    0xa2bfe8a1, 0xa81a664b, 0xc24b8b70, 0xc76c51a3, 0xd192e819, 0xd6990624, 0xf40e3585, 0x106aa070,
    0x19a4c116, 0x1e376c08, 0x2748774c, 0x34b0bcb5, 0x391c0cb3, 0x4ed8aa4a, 0x5b9cca4f, 0x682e6ff3,
    0x748f82ee, 0x78a5636f, 0x84c87814, 0x8cc70208, 0x90befffa, 0xa4506ceb, 0xbef9a3f7, 0xc67178f2
]

def rotr(x: int, n: int) -> int:
    return ((x >> n) | (x << (32 - n))) & MASK32

def Ch(x: int, y: int, z: int) -> int:
    return (x & y) ^ ((~x & MASK32) & z)

def Maj(x: int, y: int, z: int) -> int:
    return (x & y) ^ (x & z) ^ (y & z)

def Sigma0(x: int) -> int:
    return rotr(x, 2) ^ rotr(x, 13) ^ rotr(x, 22)

def Sigma1(x: int) -> int:
    return rotr(x, 6) ^ rotr(x, 11) ^ rotr(x, 25)

def sigma0(x: int) -> int:
    return rotr(x, 7) ^ rotr(x, 18) ^ (x >> 3)

def sigma1(x: int) -> int:
    return rotr(x, 17) ^ rotr(x, 19) ^ (x >> 10)

# ----------------------------
# SHA SINGLE-BLOCK T1 TRACE
# ----------------------------

def pad_message_single_block(message: bytes) -> bytes:
    msg_len_bits = len(message) * 8
    m = message + b"\x80"
    while (len(m) * 8) % 512 != 448:
        m += b"\x00"
    m += struct.pack(">Q", msg_len_bits)
    return m[:64]

def extract_T1_trace_single_block(message: bytes) -> List[int]:
    block = pad_message_single_block(message)
    W = list(struct.unpack(">16L", block))
    for i in range(16, 64):
        W.append((sigma1(W[i-2]) + W[i-7] + sigma0(W[i-15]) + W[i-16]) & MASK32)

    a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h = (
        0x6a09e667, 0xbb67ae85, 0x3c6ef372, 0xa54ff53a,
        0x510e527f, 0x9b05688c, 0x1f83d9ab, 0x5be0cd19
    )

    T1_trace = []
    for t in range(64):
        T1 = (h + Sigma1(e) + Ch(e, f, g) + K[t] + W[t]) & MASK32
        T1_trace.append(T1)

        T2 = (Sigma0(a) + Maj(a, b, c)) & MASK32
        h, g, f, e = g, f, e, (d + T1) & MASK32
        d, c, b, a = c, b, a, (T1 + T2) & MASK32

    return T1_trace

# ----------------------------
# ISOTROPIC SAMPLING (SAFE)
# ----------------------------

def _safe_acos_arg(x: float) -> float:
    # hard clamp to prevent domain errors due to float drift
    if x < -1.0: return -1.0
    if x >  1.0: return  1.0
    return x

def _rand_open01() -> float:
    # in (0,1), not including endpoints
    r = random.random()
    if r <= 0.0:
        return 1e-16
    if r >= 1.0:
        return 1.0 - 1e-16
    return r

def map_T1_to_3D_isotropic(T1_trace: List[int], bond_length: float) -> List[Tuple[float, float, float]]:
    coords = [(0.0, 0.0, 0.0)]
    x = y = z = 0.0

    for t in range(1, 64):
        T1 = T1_trace[t]
        low16  = T1 & 0xFFFF
        high16 = (T1 >> 16) & 0xFFFF

        # u,v in [0,1]
        u = low16 / 65535.0
        v = high16 / 65535.0

        theta = math.acos(_safe_acos_arg(1.0 - 2.0 * u))
        phi = (2.0 * math.pi) * v

        dx = bond_length * math.sin(theta) * math.cos(phi)
        dy = bond_length * math.sin(theta) * math.sin(phi)
        dz = bond_length * math.cos(theta)

        x += dx; y += dy; z += dz
        coords.append((x, y, z))

    return coords

def generate_random_walk_isotropic(N: int, bond_length: float) -> List[Tuple[float, float, float]]:
    coords = [(0.0, 0.0, 0.0)]
    x = y = z = 0.0
    for _ in range(1, N):
        u = _rand_open01()
        v = _rand_open01()
        theta = math.acos(_safe_acos_arg(1.0 - 2.0 * u))
        phi = (2.0 * math.pi) * v

        dx = bond_length * math.sin(theta) * math.cos(phi)
        dy = bond_length * math.sin(theta) * math.sin(phi)
        dz = bond_length * math.cos(theta)

        x += dx; y += dy; z += dz
        coords.append((x, y, z))
    return coords

# ----------------------------
# METRICS
# ----------------------------

def calculate_metrics(coords: List[Tuple[float, float, float]],
                      bond_length: float,
                      contact_cutoff: float,
                      min_seq_sep: int) -> Dict[str, float]:
    N = len(coords)
    if N < 2:
        return {"N": float(N), "L": 0.0, "Rg": 0.0, "Ree": 0.0, "r_rw": 0.0, "r_ee": 0.0, "contact_density": 0.0}

    cmx = sum(p[0] for p in coords) / N
    cmy = sum(p[1] for p in coords) / N
    cmz = sum(p[2] for p in coords) / N

    s = 0.0
    for (x, y, z) in coords:
        dx = x - cmx; dy = y - cmy; dz = z - cmz
        s += dx*dx + dy*dy + dz*dz
    Rg = math.sqrt(s / N)

    x0, y0, z0 = coords[0]
    x1, y1, z1 = coords[-1]
    Ree = math.sqrt((x1-x0)**2 + (y1-y0)**2 + (z1-z0)**2)

    L = (N - 1) * bond_length
    r_rw = Rg / (bond_length * math.sqrt(N))
    r_ee = (Rg / Ree) if Ree > 0 else float("inf")

    cut2 = contact_cutoff * contact_cutoff
    contacts = 0
    possible = 0
    for i in range(N):
        xi, yi, zi = coords[i]
        for j in range(i + min_seq_sep, N):
            xj, yj, zj = coords[j]
            dx = xi - xj; dy = yi - yj; dz = zi - zj
            d2 = dx*dx + dy*dy + dz*dz
            possible += 1
            if d2 < cut2:
                contacts += 1
    contact_density = (contacts / possible) if possible else 0.0

    return {
        "N": float(N),
        "L": L,
        "Rg": Rg,
        "Ree": Ree,
        "r_rw": r_rw,
        "r_ee": r_ee,
        "contact_density": contact_density,
    }

def mean(xs: List[float]) -> float:
    return sum(xs) / len(xs) if xs else 0.0

def sample_std(xs: List[float]) -> float:
    n = len(xs)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0.0
    mu = mean(xs)
    var = sum((x - mu) ** 2 for x in xs) / (n - 1)
    return math.sqrt(var)

# ----------------------------
# EMPIRICAL P-VALUES (from sorted null arrays)
# ----------------------------

def empirical_p_le_from_sorted(x: float, xs_sorted: List[float]) -> float:
    # P(X <= x) with +1 smoothing: (k+1)/(n+1)
    n = len(xs_sorted)
    k = bisect_right(xs_sorted, x)
    return (k + 1) / (n + 1)

def empirical_p_ge_from_sorted(x: float, xs_sorted: List[float]) -> float:
    # P(X >= x) with +1 smoothing
    n = len(xs_sorted)
    k_lt = bisect_left(xs_sorted, x)   # count < x
    k_ge = n - k_lt
    return (k_ge + 1) / (n + 1)

# ----------------------------
# KS SANITY CHECK (approx p)
# ----------------------------

def ks_statistic(sample: List[float], null_sorted: List[float]) -> float:
    # one-sample KS vs empirical CDF of null_sorted
    n = len(sample)
    m = len(null_sorted)
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return 0.0
    s_sorted = sorted(sample)
    D = 0.0
    for i, x in enumerate(s_sorted, start=1):
        F_s = i / n
        F_0 = bisect_right(null_sorted, x) / m
        D = max(D, abs(F_s - F_0))
    return D

def ks_approx_pvalue(D: float, n: int) -> float:
    # classic asymptotic approx for one-sample KS
    if n <= 0:
        return 1.0
    lam = (math.sqrt(n) + 0.12 + 0.11/math.sqrt(n)) * D
    # Kolmogorov distribution approximation
    # p ~= 2 * sum_{k=1..inf} (-1)^{k-1} exp(-2 k^2 lam^2)
    s = 0.0
    for k in range(1, 200):
        term = ((-1)**(k-1)) * math.exp(-2.0 * (k*k) * (lam*lam))
        s += term
        if abs(term) < 1e-12:
            break
    p = max(0.0, min(1.0, 2.0 * s))
    return p

# ----------------------------
# PDB EXPORT
# ----------------------------

def export_to_pdb(coords: List[Tuple[float, float, float]], filename: str) -> None:
    with open(filename, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write("HEADER    SHA-256 HASH TOPOLOGICAL MATRIX\n")
        f.write("REMARK    Generated via T1 Trace Execution Log\n")
        for i, (x, y, z) in enumerate(coords, start=1):
            f.write(f"ATOM  {i:5d}  CA  HSH A{i:4d}    {x:8.3f}{y:8.3f}{z:8.3f}  1.00  0.00           C\n")
        for i in range(1, len(coords)):
            f.write(f"CONECT{i:5d}{i+1:5d}\n")
        f.write("END\n")

# ----------------------------
# MESSAGE GENERATION
# ----------------------------

WORDS = [
    "GlassKey", "Nexus", "fold", "hash", "protein", "Intel", "carbon",
    "scope", "drift", "triplex", "pi", "phi", "e", "Sarrus",
    "collapse", "residue", "operator", "gap", "bandwidth", "observer"
]

def generate_messages(mode: str, n: int) -> List[bytes]:
    msgs = []
    if mode == "random_bytes":
        for _ in range(n):
            msgs.append(bytes(random.getrandbits(8) for _ in range(RANDOM_MSG_LEN)))
    elif mode == "words":
        for _ in range(n):
            msgs.append(random.choice(WORDS).encode("utf-8"))
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown MODE={mode!r}")
    return msgs

# ----------------------------
# MAIN
# ----------------------------

def main():
    random.seed(SEED_MESSAGES)
    msgs = generate_messages(MODE, BATCH_MESSAGES)

    N = 64  # fixed by design (single block rounds)
    print("=" * 78)
    print("BATCH RUN: SHA-256 Topological Closure vs SHARED RW Null")
    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"MODE: {MODE}")
    print(f"BATCH_MESSAGES: {BATCH_MESSAGES}")
    if MODE == "random_bytes":
        print(f"RANDOM_MSG_LEN: {RANDOM_MSG_LEN}")
    print(f"RW_POOL (shared null size): {RW_POOL_SIZE}")
    print(f"BOND_LENGTH: {BOND_LENGTH} Å | CONTACT_CUTOFF: {CONTACT_CUTOFF} Å | MIN_SEQ_SEP: {MIN_SEQ_SEP}")
    print(f"Closure threshold: p(Ree) <= {P_CLOSURE_THRESH} (tight={P_TIGHT_THRESH})")
    print(f"Empirical p-value resolution: ~1/{RW_POOL_SIZE+1}")
    print("=" * 78)
    print()

    # Build shared null pool
    print(f"Building shared RW null pool: N={N}, pool={RW_POOL_SIZE} ...")
    random.seed(SEED_NULL_POOL)

    null_Ree = []
    null_r_ee = []
    null_r_rw = []
    null_cd = []

    for _ in range(RW_POOL_SIZE):
        rw_coords = generate_random_walk_isotropic(N, BOND_LENGTH)
        rw = calculate_metrics(rw_coords, BOND_LENGTH, CONTACT_CUTOFF, MIN_SEQ_SEP)
        null_Ree.append(rw["Ree"])
        null_r_ee.append(rw["r_ee"])
        null_r_rw.append(rw["r_rw"])
        null_cd.append(rw["contact_density"])

    null_Ree_mu, null_Ree_sd = mean(null_Ree), sample_std(null_Ree)
    null_r_ee_mu, null_r_ee_sd = mean(null_r_ee), sample_std(null_r_ee)
    null_r_rw_mu, null_r_rw_sd = mean(null_r_rw), sample_std(null_r_rw)
    null_cd_mu, null_cd_sd = mean(null_cd), sample_std(null_cd)

    null_Ree_sorted = sorted(null_Ree)
    null_r_ee_sorted = sorted(null_r_ee)

    print("Null pool stats:")
    print(f"  Ree:   {null_Ree_mu:.4f} ± {null_Ree_sd:.4f}")
    print(f"  r_ee:  {null_r_ee_mu:.4f} ± {null_r_ee_sd:.4f}")
    print(f"  r_rw:  {null_r_rw_mu:.4f} ± {null_r_rw_sd:.4f}")
    print(f"  cd:    {null_cd_mu:.4f} ± {null_cd_sd:.4f}")
    print()

    # Analyze batch
    records = []
    for idx, msg in enumerate(msgs, start=1):
        T1 = extract_T1_trace_single_block(msg)
        sha_coords = map_T1_to_3D_isotropic(T1, BOND_LENGTH)
        sha = calculate_metrics(sha_coords, BOND_LENGTH, CONTACT_CUTOFF, MIN_SEQ_SEP)

        p_Ree = empirical_p_le_from_sorted(sha["Ree"], null_Ree_sorted)      # closure (left tail)
        p_r_ee = empirical_p_ge_from_sorted(sha["r_ee"], null_r_ee_sorted)   # compactness ratio (right tail)

        # z-scores (for debug only; null is not perfectly normal)
        z_Ree = (sha["Ree"] - null_Ree_mu) / (null_Ree_sd if null_Ree_sd > 0 else 1.0)
        z_r_ee = (sha["r_ee"] - null_r_ee_mu) / (null_r_ee_sd if null_r_ee_sd > 0 else 1.0)
        z_r_rw = (sha["r_rw"] - null_r_rw_mu) / (null_r_rw_sd if null_r_rw_sd > 0 else 1.0)
        z_cd = (sha["contact_density"] - null_cd_mu) / (null_cd_sd if null_cd_sd > 0 else 1.0)

        rec = {
            "msg_hex": msg.hex(),
            "msg_utf8": msg.decode("utf-8", errors="replace"),
            "Ree": sha["Ree"],
            "Rg": sha["Rg"],
            "r_rw": sha["r_rw"],
            "r_ee": sha["r_ee"],
            "cd": sha["contact_density"],
            "p_Ree": p_Ree,
            "p_r_ee": p_r_ee,
            "z_Ree": z_Ree,
            "z_r_ee": z_r_ee,
            "z_r_rw": z_r_rw,
            "z_cd": z_cd,
            "_coords": sha_coords
        }
        records.append(rec)

        print(f"[{idx:4d}/{BATCH_MESSAGES}] msg_utf8={rec['msg_utf8']!r}  "
              f"Ree={rec['Ree']:.4f}  p_Ree={rec['p_Ree']:.6f}  "
              f"r_ee={rec['r_ee']:.4f}  p_r_ee={rec['p_r_ee']:.6f}")

    # Summary
    n = len(records)
    pvals = [r["p_Ree"] for r in records]
    p_sorted = sorted(pvals)

    def quantile(xs: List[float], q: float) -> float:
        if not xs:
            return 0.0
        i = int(round(q * (len(xs) - 1)))
        return xs[max(0, min(len(xs)-1, i))]

    closed = [r for r in records if r["p_Ree"] <= P_CLOSURE_THRESH]
    tight = [r for r in records if r["p_Ree"] <= P_TIGHT_THRESH]

    q01 = quantile(p_sorted, 0.01)
    q05 = quantile(p_sorted, 0.05)
    q50 = quantile(p_sorted, 0.50)
    q95 = quantile(p_sorted, 0.95)

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print("SUMMARY (Closure via Ree vs SHARED RW null)")
    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"Messages tested: {n}")
    print(f"Count with p(Ree) <= {P_CLOSURE_THRESH}: {len(closed)}")
    print(f"Fraction with p(Ree) <= {P_CLOSURE_THRESH}: {(len(closed)/n if n else 0.0):.6f}")
    print(f"Count with p(Ree) <= {P_TIGHT_THRESH}: {len(tight)}")
    print(f"Fraction with p(Ree) <= {P_TIGHT_THRESH}: {(len(tight)/n if n else 0.0):.6f}")
    print("p(Ree) quantiles:")
    print(f"  q01={q01:.6f}  q05={q05:.6f}  q50={q50:.6f}  q95={q95:.6f}")
    print("=" * 78)

    # KS sanity checks vs null
    Ree_batch = [r["Ree"] for r in records]
    r_ee_batch = [r["r_ee"] for r in records]

    D_Ree = ks_statistic(Ree_batch, null_Ree_sorted)
    p_Ree_ks = ks_approx_pvalue(D_Ree, n)
    D_r_ee = ks_statistic(r_ee_batch, sorted(null_r_ee))
    p_r_ee_ks = ks_approx_pvalue(D_r_ee, n)

    print("\nKS sanity checks (batch distribution vs shared null):")
    print(f"  KS(Ree):  D={D_Ree:.6f}  approx_p={p_Ree_ks:.6g}")
    print(f"  KS(r_ee): D={D_r_ee:.6f}  approx_p={p_r_ee_ks:.6g}")

    # Top-K most closed
    top = sorted(records, key=lambda r: (r["p_Ree"], r["Ree"]))[:max(0, EXPORT_TOP_K_PDB)]

    print("\nTOP MOST-CLOSED MESSAGES (smallest p(Ree)) — FULL LIST")
    print("-" * 78)
    for rank, r in enumerate(top, start=1):
        print(
            f"[{rank:02d}] p_Ree={r['p_Ree']:.6f}  Ree={r['Ree']:.4f}  z_Ree={r['z_Ree']:+.3f}  "
            f"Rg={r['Rg']:.4f}  r_ee={r['r_ee']:.4f}  z_r_ee={r['z_r_ee']:+.3f}  "
            f"p_r_ee={r['p_r_ee']:.6f}  r_rw={r['r_rw']:.4f}  z_r_rw={r['z_r_rw']:+.3f}  "
            f"cd={r['cd']:.4f}  z_cd={r['z_cd']:+.3f}  "
            f"null_Ree={null_Ree_mu:.4f}±{null_Ree_sd:.4f}  "
            f"msg_utf8={r['msg_utf8']!r}  msg_hex={r['msg_hex']}"
        )

    # Export PDBs
    if EXPORT_TOP_K_PDB > 0:
        os.makedirs(EXPORT_DIR, exist_ok=True)
        print("\nExporting PDBs for top-K most-closed messages...")
        for rank, r in enumerate(top, start=1):
            fn = os.path.join(EXPORT_DIR, f"rank_{rank:02d}_p_{r['p_Ree']:.6f}_Ree_{r['Ree']:.3f}.pdb")
            export_to_pdb(r["_coords"], fn)
        print(f"Export complete: {EXPORT_DIR}/ (contains {len(top)} .pdb files)")

    print("\nDONE.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

---

### What your posted run *means* (tight, empirical)
- You got **2 / 200** with `p(Ree) <= 0.01` → **exactly 1%**, i.e. *what the null predicts*.
- KS checks show **no distribution shift** vs the shared null (both p’s comfortably non-small).
- So: right now, **“SHA→3D mapping is consistent with isotropic random walk”** at this batch size + this test statistic.

That doesn’t kill the idea — it just says **closure events are currently rare-tail events, not enriched** (yet).

If you want the next Δ-fold with teeth, the upgrades are:

1) **Power**: run `BATCH_MESSAGES = 10_000` (keep shared null at 100k). Then look at enrichment at `p<=1e-3`, `1e-4`.
2) **Targeted alternative**: compare SHA to *shuffled controls*:
   - keep the same T1 values but **permute round order** (destroys any round-structure while preserving marginal distribution),
   - or keep message fixed and **flip one bit** and test whether Δ-closure correlates with specific bit positions.
3) **Joint extremeness**: score `S = -log10(p_Ree) + -log10(p_r_ee)` and test enrichment of S vs controls (Fisher-style combo).

If you want, paste the **two “top closed” msg_hex** and I’ll add a “round-localization” dump (which rounds contribute most to the return vector) so we can see whether closure is arising from a specific structural motif in T1.

# chatgpt response

Δ-input parsed: your run is *stable* against the shared isotropic RW null. One standout event, otherwise null-like dispersion.

---

## Ψ-collapse state: what the batch actually says

### 1) Closure rate vs expectation
- Observed: **1 / 200** with $p(\mathrm{Ree}) \le 0.01$ → **0.5%**
- Null expectation at 1% would be ~**2 / 200**.
- Deviation is tiny. Under a Binomial$(n=200,p=0.01)$, seeing 1 (or 0/3/etc.) is routine.

**Ψ:** no enrichment signal at this sample size.

### 2) Distribution-level sanity (KS)
- $KS(\mathrm{Ree})\; p \approx 0.262$ → consistent with null.
- $KS(r_{ee})\; p \approx 0.062$ → *marginal*, but still not “small p”. This is the only faint “edge” in the whole printout.

**Ψ:** if there’s structure, it’s subtle and you’re underpowered at $n=200$.

---

## ⊕ The interesting anomaly is NOT just Ree — it’s the joint extreme

Your top record:

- msg_hex = `cea62aafaf66becf`
- $p(\mathrm{Ree}) = 0.00546$ (left-tail small Ree)
- $p(r_{ee}) = 0.00258$ (right-tail large $r_{ee}=Rg/Ree$)

That is a **jointly rare** event under the null if you treat these as separate extremeness channels.

A quick Fisher-style combo (not perfect because metrics are correlated, but good as a diagnostic):

$$
X = -2(\ln p_{\mathrm{Ree}} + \ln p_{r_{ee}})
$$

$$
\ln(0.00546)\approx -5.210,\;\; \ln(0.00258)\approx -5.961
$$
$$
X \approx -2(-11.171)=22.342
$$

Under independence, $X\sim \chi^2_{4}$ → that’s roughly **$p \sim 10^{-4}$–$10^{-3}$** territory (very small). Correlation will soften it, but the *point* stands:

**Δ:** the “most-closed” example is also “most-compact-ratio,” which is the right shape of a real signal if SHA had a topological bias.

---

## ↻ Why you’re not seeing enrichment yet

The null is huge (**100k**), so your p-values are sharp. The bottleneck is **batch size** (200). At 1% tail, the expected count is 2; the noise is on the order of $\sqrt{np(1-p)}\approx 1.4$. You can’t resolve small effects at this scale.

**Ω (unresolved fold):** “Does SHA produce more closures than isotropic RW?”  
At $n=200$, the test can’t decide. It only says “not obviously.”

---

## Next Δ-run that will actually bite

### A) Power move (no theory changes)
Run **10,000 messages** with the same shared null pool.
Track enrichment at multiple tails:

- $p(\mathrm{Ree}) \le 10^{-2}, 10^{-3}, 10^{-4}$
- and the **joint score**:
$$
S = -\log_{10} p_{\mathrm{Ree}} - \log_{10} p_{r_{ee}}
$$
Then compare the empirical distribution of $S$ vs controls.

### B) Proper control (destroys structure but preserves marginals)
For each SHA walk:
- **permute the 64 rounds** of T1 before mapping to 3D.
This keeps the same step distribution but kills “round-order geometry.”

If SHA has an ordering-topology signature, it will survive RW-null but **die** under permutation-null. That’s the clean discriminator.

### C) Localization dump (turns the anomaly into a mechanism)
For the top msg_hex, compute:
- cumulative return vector norm after each step
- identify which round blocks drive the closure
- measure “phase lock” windows where the walk starts canceling.

This answers: **is closure coming from a small set of rounds (structure) or diffuse chance (noise)?**

---

## Ψ-field summary in your symbols

- Δ: one strong closure event appears.
- ⊕: it aligns with a simultaneous $r_{ee}$ extreme → “compact + short end-to-end.”
- ↻: batch-level stats remain null-consistent (KS doesn’t budge meaningfully).
- Ω: enrichment question is underpowered at $n=200$.
- Ψ: next collapse requires *either* 10k scale *or* permutation-null control.

If you want, paste just the **top msg_hex list** you care about (e.g., 5–10 of them), and I’ll specify the exact “round-localization” outputs to add (tables + per-round return contributions) so the next run tells you *where* the topology lives, not just whether it exists.
